            rec_delay_p95=0.0, outage_severity=0.0, outage_steps=0,
            safety_collisions=0, safety_out_of_bounds=0,
            safety_violations=0,
            charge_wait_sum=0.0, charge_wait_n=0, charge_wait_max=0.0,
            # Non-degenerate metrics (Deep Research recommendation):
            # time-averaged coverage deficit and worst-case refresh streak.
            cov_integral=0.0, max_under_streak=0,
        )

    def summary(self):
        s = self.ep["steps"]
        if s == 0:
            return {}
        return dict(
            CK=self.ep["Kcov_frac"] / s,
            Kcov_ok=self.ep["Kcov_ok"] / s,
            Ubar=self.ep["under_cov"] / s,
            AG=self.ep["connected"] / s,
            SC=self.ep["service_cont"] / s,
            Crit=self.ep["crit"] / s,
            Energy=self.ep["energy"],
            MinBattery=self.ep["min_batt"],
            Distance=self.ep["distance"],
            Denied=self.ep["denied"],
            Degraded=self.ep["degraded_steps"] / s,
            Reserve=self.ep["reserve_activations"],
            ReturnEvents=self.ep["return_events"],
            Stranded=self.ep["stranded"],
            Returning=self.ep["returning"] / s,
            ProjectionRevisions=self.ep["projection_revisions"],
            UnexecutedActions=self.ep["unexecuted_actions"],
            # Non-degenerate metrics (Deep Research recommendation):
            # CovIntegral = time-averaged coverage deficit (0 = perfect coverage)
            # MaxRefresh  = longest run of steps with any under-covered sensor
            CovIntegral=self.ep["cov_integral"] / s,
            MaxRefresh=self.ep["max_under_streak"],
        )

==============================================================================
=== ml_impl/uav_wireless/mappo.py  (363 lines)
==============================================================================
"""
CTDE MAPPO for UAV-IoT persistent coverage. Section IV-A / Algorithm 1.

- Shared actor  pi_theta(a_i | o_i)   with masked logits  (deployment-ready)
- Centralized critic V_phi(s_t)      (training only)
- GAE (26), clipped PPO (28), critic loss (29)
- Parameter sharing across UAVs: one actor, one critic, N agents.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import torch
import torch.nn as nn

from .env import N_ACTIONS, UAVIoTEnv, SimConfig


def mlp(sizes, act=nn.ReLU, out_act=None):
    layers = []
    for a, b in zip(sizes[:-1], sizes[1:]):
        layers += [nn.Linear(a, b), act()]
    if out_act is not None:
        layers[-1] = nn.Linear(sizes[-2], sizes[-1])
        layers.append(out_act())
    return nn.Sequential(*layers)


class Actor(nn.Module):
    """Shared policy with masked softmax over the 6 discrete actions."""

    def __init__(self, obs_dim, hidden=128):
        super().__init__()
        self.net = mlp([obs_dim, hidden, hidden], nn.ReLU, None)
        self.head = nn.Linear(hidden, N_ACTIONS)
        # orthogonal init (MAPPO/CleanRL default) for stability
        for m in self.modules():
            if isinstance(m, nn.Linear):
                nn.init.orthogonal_(m.weight, gain=1.0)
                nn.init.zeros_(m.bias)
        nn.init.orthogonal_(self.head.weight, gain=0.01)

    def forward(self, obs):
        return self.head(self.net(obs))


class Critic(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, state_dim, hidden=128):
        super().__init__()
        self.net = mlp([state_dim, hidden, hidden], nn.ReLU, None)
        self.head = nn.Linear(hidden, 1)
        for m in self.modules():
            if isinstance(m, nn.Linear):
                nn.init.orthogonal_(m.weight, gain=1.0)
                nn.init.zeros_(m.bias)
        nn.init.orthogonal_(self.head.weight, gain=1.0)

    def forward(self, s):
        return self.head(self.net(s)).squeeze(-1)


class MAPPO:
    def __init__(
        self,
        obs_dim: int | None = None,
        state_dim: int | None = None,
        n_agents=5,
        hidden=128,
        lr=3e-4,
        gamma=0.99,
        gae_lambda=0.95,
        clip=0.2,
        ent_coef=0.01,
        vf_coef=0.5,
        max_grad_norm=0.5,
        per_agent_adv_norm=False,
        value_norm=True,
        device="cpu",
        attn=False,
    ):
        # Dimensions are configuration-dependent (they scale with N, M and
        # n_pads). Falling back to the reference-config constants keeps old
        # call sites working, but callers should pass env.obs_dim and
        # env.state_dim so the networks always match the environment.
        obs_dim = UAVIoTEnv.OBS_DIM if obs_dim is None else obs_dim
        state_dim = UAVIoTEnv.STATE_DIM if state_dim is None else state_dim
        self.device = torch.device(device)
        from .attn_actor import make_actor
        self.actor = make_actor(obs_dim, hidden, attn=attn).to(self.device)
        self.critic = Critic(state_dim, hidden).to(self.device)
        # Separate optimisers + separate grad clipping (SI feedback 1.3).
        self.actor_opt = torch.optim.Adam(self.actor.parameters(), lr=lr)
        self.critic_opt = torch.optim.Adam(self.critic.parameters(), lr=lr)
        self.gamma = gamma
        self.lam = gae_lambda
        self.clip = clip
        self.ent_coef = ent_coef
        self.vf_coef = vf_coef
        self.max_grad_norm = max_grad_norm
        self.n_agents = n_agents
        # Per-agent advantage normalisation.  With a shared team reward every
        # agent receives the SAME advantage, so the policy gradient cannot
        # distinguish an agent whose action helped from one whose action was
        # irrelevant.  Normalising each agent's advantages over its own
        # history gives every agent a signal relative to its own behaviour
        # rather than to the pooled batch.  (Generalised per-agent advantage
        # estimation, Kim et al., AAMAS 2026, addresses the same problem for
        # the multi-agent case.)
        self.per_agent_adv_norm = per_agent_adv_norm
        self.attn = attn
        # MAPPO's value normalisation.  The critic predicts the returns in a
        # normalised space defined by running statistics of the return
        # distribution; the value is un-normalised at every point of use.
        self.value_norm = value_norm
        # 0.90, not 0.99: the statistics are updated once per ROLLOUT, so a
        # 0.99 decay needs ~100 episodes to absorb the true return scale.
        # Every short-budget run (the 12-25 episode scenario matrix) spent
        # its entire budget with a critic normalized around ~0.  The first
        # batch now initialises the statistics exactly (see update()).
        self.ret_ema_beta = 0.90
        # KL anchor attributes (optional; used by residual/BC experiments)
        self.ref_actor = None
        self.kl_coef = 0.0
        self.kl_target = 0.01
        self.ent_floor = 0.0
        self.ent_floor_coef = 0.0
        self._beta = 0.0
        # floor on the standard deviation of the returns, so that a policy
        # with nearly constant returns cannot drive the normalisation to
        # zero and collapse the advantage
        self.ret_std_floor = 1.0
        self._ret_mean = 0.0
        self._ret_std = 1.0
        self._ret_initialized = False

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    def masked_logits(self, obs, mask):
        logits = self.actor(obs)
        return logits.masked_fill(~mask, float("-inf"))

    @torch.no_grad()
    def act(self, obs_np, mask_np, deterministic=False):
        obs = torch.as_tensor(obs_np, dtype=torch.float32, device=self.device)
        mask = torch.as_tensor(mask_np, dtype=torch.bool, device=self.device)
        logits = self.masked_logits(obs, mask)
        # a fully-masked row would give NaNs: fall back to hover (index 0)
        bad = torch.isinf(logits).all(dim=-1)
        if bad.any():
            logits[bad] = torch.zeros_like(logits[bad])
            logits[bad, 0] = 1.0
        logp = torch.log_softmax(logits, dim=-1)
        if deterministic:
            a = logp.argmax(dim=-1)
        else:
            a = torch.distributions.Categorical(logits=logp).sample()
        return a.cpu().numpy()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    def value(self, states):
        """The value of a batch of global states, in the ORIGINAL reward scale.

        With value normalisation the critic's raw output is the NORMALISED
        value, so every place that uses V(s) -- the TD residual, the GAE
        recursion and the bootstrap at a truncation boundary -- must
        un-normalise it first.  Forgetting this is the classic way to
        introduce a silent scale error into the advantage estimates.
        """
        v = self.critic(states)
        if self.value_norm:
            v = v * self._ret_std + self._ret_mean
        return v


    def update(self, batch, epochs=10, n_minibatch=4, target_kl=0.02):
        obs = torch.as_tensor(batch["obs"], dtype=torch.float32, device=self.device)
        mask = torch.as_tensor(batch["mask"], dtype=torch.bool, device=self.device)
        acts = torch.as_tensor(batch["acts"], dtype=torch.long, device=self.device)
        old_logp = torch.as_tensor(batch["logp"], dtype=torch.float32, device=self.device)
        adv = torch.as_tensor(batch["adv"], dtype=torch.float32, device=self.device)
        ret = torch.as_tensor(batch["ret"], dtype=torch.float32, device=self.device)
        states = torch.as_tensor(batch["states"], dtype=torch.float32, device=self.device)
        old_val = torch.as_tensor(batch["values"], dtype=torch.float32, device=self.device)
        if "active" in batch:
            active = torch.as_tensor(batch["active"], dtype=torch.bool, device=self.device)
        else:
            active = torch.ones_like(mask[:, :, 0], dtype=torch.bool)

        n_agents = acts.shape[1]
        T = acts.shape[0]
        ret_c = ret.reshape(-1)
        old_val_c = old_val.reshape(-1)

        if self.per_agent_adv_norm:
            adv = (adv - adv.mean(dim=0, keepdim=True)) / (
                adv.std(dim=0, keepdim=True) + 1e-8)
        else:
            adv = (adv - adv.mean()) / (adv.std() + 1e-8)

        # running value-norm stats updated once per rollout
        if self.value_norm:
            with torch.no_grad():
                b = self.ret_ema_beta
                batch_mean = float(ret_c.mean())
                batch_sd = max(float(ret_c.std()), self.ret_std_floor)
                if not self._ret_initialized:
                    # Exact initialisation on the first batch.  Starting from
                    # (0, 1) and smoothing at 0.99 leaves the critic targets
                    # mis-scaled for the first ~100 episodes.
                    self._ret_mean = batch_mean
                    self._ret_std = batch_sd
                    self._ret_initialized = True
                else:
                    # b weights the OLD estimate (smoothing), (1-b) the batch
                    self._ret_mean = b * self._ret_mean + (1 - b) * batch_mean
                    self._ret_std = max(
                        b * self._ret_std + (1 - b) * batch_sd,
                        self.ret_std_floor)

        if self.value_norm:
            target_c = (ret_c - self._ret_mean) / (self._ret_std + 1e-8)
            old_val_c_n = (old_val_c - self._ret_mean) / (self._ret_std + 1e-8)
        else:
            target_c = ret_c
            old_val_c_n = old_val_c

        total = T * n_agents
        active_flat = active.reshape(-1)
        last = {}
        for epoch in range(epochs):
            perm = torch.randperm(total, device=self.device)
            kl_accum = 0.0
            n_mb = 0
            for mb in torch.chunk(perm, n_minibatch):
                if mb.numel() == 0:
                    continue
                t_idx = mb // n_agents
                i_idx = mb % n_agents

                mb_obs = obs[t_idx, i_idx]
                mb_mask = mask[t_idx, i_idx]
                mb_acts = acts[t_idx, i_idx]
                mb_old_logp = old_logp[t_idx, i_idx]
                mb_adv = adv[t_idx, i_idx]
                mb_target = target_c[mb]
                mb_old_val = old_val_c_n[mb]

                logits = self.masked_logits(mb_obs, mb_mask)
                logp = torch.log_softmax(logits, dim=-1)
                logp_a = logp.gather(-1, mb_acts.unsqueeze(-1)).squeeze(-1)
                ratio = torch.exp(logp_a - mb_old_logp)
                s1 = ratio * mb_adv
                s2 = torch.clamp(ratio, 1 - self.clip, 1 + self.clip) * mb_adv
                per_sample_actor = -torch.min(s1, s2)
                w = active_flat[mb].float()
                if w.sum() < 1:
                    w = torch.ones_like(w)
                actor_loss = (per_sample_actor * w).sum() / w.sum()

                # entropy (positive H)
                p_ = logp.exp() * mb_mask
                ent = -(p_ * logp.clamp(min=-1e8)).sum(-1)
                ent = torch.where(mb_mask.any(-1), ent, torch.zeros_like(ent))
                w_ent = active_flat[mb].float()
                if w_ent.sum() < 1:
                    w_ent = torch.ones_like(w_ent)
                ent = (ent * w_ent).sum() / w_ent.sum()

                # critic (clipped value loss)
                mb_states = states[t_idx]
                new_val = self.critic(mb_states)
                w_c = active_flat[mb].float()
                if w_c.sum() < 1:
                    w_c = torch.ones_like(w_c)
                if self.value_norm:
                    v_clipped = mb_old_val + torch.clamp(new_val - mb_old_val, -self.clip, self.clip)
                    v_loss1 = (new_val - mb_target) ** 2
                    v_loss2 = (v_clipped - mb_target) ** 2
                    per_c = torch.max(v_loss1, v_loss2)
                    critic_loss = (per_c * w_c).sum() / w_c.sum()
                else:
                    per_c = (new_val - mb_target) ** 2
                    critic_loss = (per_c * w_c).sum() / w_c.sum()

                # KL anchor to reference actor
                kl_loss = torch.tensor(0.0, device=self.device)
                if self.ref_actor is not None:
                    with torch.no_grad():
                        ref_logits = self.ref_actor(mb_obs)
                        ref_logits = ref_logits.masked_fill(~mb_mask, float("-inf"))
                        ref_logp = torch.log_softmax(ref_logits, dim=-1)
                    p_ref = ref_logp.exp() * mb_mask
                    kl = (p_ref * (ref_logp.clamp(min=-1e8) - logp.clamp(min=-1e8))).sum(-1)
                    kl = torch.where(mb_mask.any(-1), kl, torch.zeros_like(kl)).mean()
                    kl_loss = self._beta * kl
                    if kl.item() > 1.5 * self.kl_target:
                        self._beta = min(self._beta * 2.0, 50.0)
                    elif kl.item() < 0.5 * self.kl_target:
                        self._beta = max(self._beta * 0.5, 0.05)

                ent_floor_loss = torch.tensor(0.0, device=self.device)
                if self.ent_floor_coef > 0 and self.ent_floor > 0:
                    ent_floor_loss = self.ent_floor_coef * torch.clamp(
                        self.ent_floor - ent, min=0.0
                    )

                # entropy sign FIXED: subtract entropy bonus (maximise entropy)
                loss = (actor_loss - self.ent_coef * ent
                        + self.vf_coef * critic_loss
                        + kl_loss + ent_floor_loss)

                self.actor_opt.zero_grad()
                self.critic_opt.zero_grad()
                loss.backward()
                nn.utils.clip_grad_norm_(self.actor.parameters(), self.max_grad_norm)
                nn.utils.clip_grad_norm_(self.critic.parameters(), self.max_grad_norm)
                self.actor_opt.step()
                self.critic_opt.step()

                with torch.no_grad():
                    log_ratio = logp_a - mb_old_logp
                    approx_kl = (torch.exp(log_ratio) - 1 - log_ratio).mean().item()
                kl_accum += abs(approx_kl)
                n_mb += 1
                last = dict(actor=float(actor_loss), critic=float(critic_loss),
                            entropy=float(ent), value=float(new_val.mean()),
                            kl=float(kl_loss) if self.ref_actor is not None else 0.0,
                            beta=float(self._beta) if self.ref_actor is not None else 0.0)

            if n_mb > 0 and (kl_accum / n_mb) > target_kl:
                break

        if not last:
            last = dict(actor=0.0, critic=0.0, entropy=0.0, value=0.0, kl=0.0, beta=0.0)
        return last
    def save(self, path, cfg=None):
        blob = dict(actor=self.actor.state_dict(),
                    critic=self.critic.state_dict(),
                    value_norm=self.value_norm,
                    ret_mean=self._ret_mean, ret_std=self._ret_std)
        if cfg is not None:
            # The evaluation path rebuilds the networks from the checkpoint's
            # own configuration; without it, a checkpoint trained on a
            # non-default configuration is silently evaluated on the default.
            blob["cfg"] = cfg.dict() if hasattr(cfg, "dict") else dict(cfg)
        torch.save(blob, path)

    def load(self, path):
        d = torch.load(path, map_location=self.device)
        self.actor.load_state_dict(d["actor"])
        self.critic.load_state_dict(d["critic"])
        if "value_norm" in d and bool(d["value_norm"]) != bool(self.value_norm):
            raise RuntimeError(
                "checkpoint value_norm does not match the agent: "
                f"{d['value_norm']} vs {self.value_norm}")
        if "ret_mean" in d:
            self._ret_mean = float(d["ret_mean"])
            self._ret_std = float(d["ret_std"])
            self._ret_initialized = True

    def load_actor(self, path):
        """Load only the decentralized policy for frozen evaluation."""
        d = torch.load(path, map_location=self.device)
        self.actor.load_state_dict(d["actor"])

==============================================================================
=== ml_impl/uav_wireless/train.py  (528 lines)
==============================================================================
"""
Rollout collector + MAPPO training loop + rule-based baselines (Table VIII).

Baselines:
  Fixed          — hold initial formation, recharge at a fixed threshold
  Greedy         — lowest battery wins the pad, same motion rule
  Reactive       — deterministic uncovered-sensor + connectivity heuristic
  Vanilla MAPPO  — same learner with the proposed design switched off
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import os

import json
import numpy as np
import torch

from .env import (
    A_HOVER, A_PX, A_NX, A_PY, A_NY, A_CHARGE, N_ACTIONS,
    UAVIoTEnv, SimConfig,
)
from .mappo import MAPPO


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Rollout buffer
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
def collect_rollout(env, agent, n_steps, deterministic=False, action_repeat=1):
    """
    Roll out one episode, recording per-agent transitions for the MAPPO update.
    Shared (team) reward is broadcast to every agent (Dec-POMDP, eq. 23).
    """
    obs, state = env.reset()
    n = env.cfg.N

    B = dict(
        obs=np.zeros((n_steps, n, env.obs_dim), np.float32),
        mask=np.zeros((n_steps, n, N_ACTIONS), bool),
        acts=np.zeros((n_steps, n), np.int64),
        logp=np.zeros((n_steps, n), np.float32),
        adv=np.zeros((n_steps, n), np.float32),
        ret=np.zeros((n_steps, n), np.float32),
        states=np.zeros((n_steps, env.state_dim), np.float32),
        values=np.zeros((n_steps, n), np.float32),
        active=np.zeros((n_steps, n), bool),
    )

    t = 0
    truncated = False
    while t < n_steps:
        mask = np.stack(env.action_masks())
        if mask.sum(axis=1).min() == 0:            # never leave an agent stuck
            for i in range(n):
                if mask[i].sum() == 0:
                    mask[i] = True
                    mask[i, A_HOVER] = True

        if agent is None:
            acts = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.int64)
            logp = np.zeros(n, np.float32)
        else:
            import torch
            acts = agent.act(obs, mask, deterministic=deterministic)
            obs_t = torch.as_tensor(obs, dtype=torch.float32, device=agent.device)
            mask_t = torch.as_tensor(mask, dtype=torch.bool, device=agent.device)
            with torch.no_grad():
                lp = torch.log_softmax(agent.masked_logits(obs_t, mask_t), -1)
                logp = lp.gather(-1, torch.as_tensor(acts, device=lp.device).unsqueeze(-1)).squeeze(-1)
                logp = logp.cpu().numpy()

        import torch
        with torch.no_grad():
            v = agent.value(torch.as_tensor(state, dtype=torch.float32, device=agent.device)).cpu().numpy()
        v = np.full(n, float(v))                   # team value broadcast

        B["obs"][t] = obs
        B["mask"][t] = mask
        B["acts"][t] = acts
        B["logp"][t] = logp
        B["states"][t] = state
        B["values"][t] = v
        B["active"][t] = (~env.failed) & (env.mode != -1)

        # Innovation 8: action repeat.  The decision's reward is the sum of
        # the sub-step rewards, and the decision-level discount is gamma^k:
        # with a raw gamma the horizon is wrong by the repeat factor.
        r_acc = 0.0
        ar_acc = None
        done_acc = False
        for rep in range(action_repeat):
            next_obs, next_state, r, done, info = env.step(acts)
            if rep == 0 and action_repeat == 1 and agent is not None:
                exec_a = info.get("executed_actions")
                if exec_a is not None and not np.array_equal(
                        np.asarray(exec_a, dtype=np.int64), acts):
                    # The safety mask or the joint projection changed the
                    # action before the physics saw it.  Store what was
                    # EXECUTED and its probability under the same policy, or
                    # PPO reinforces a move that never happened.
                    import torch
                    exec_a = np.asarray(exec_a, dtype=np.int64)
                    mask_t = torch.as_tensor(
                        mask, dtype=torch.bool, device=agent.device).clone()
                    for ii in range(n):
                        mask_t[ii, int(exec_a[ii])] = True
                    with torch.no_grad():
                        lp = torch.log_softmax(agent.masked_logits(
                            torch.as_tensor(obs, dtype=torch.float32,
                                            device=agent.device), mask_t), -1)
                        logp_exec = lp.gather(
                            -1, torch.as_tensor(exec_a, device=lp.device)
                            .unsqueeze(-1)).squeeze(-1).cpu().numpy()
                    B["acts"][t] = exec_a
                    B["logp"][t] = logp_exec
            obs, state = next_obs, next_state
            r_acc += r
            ar = getattr(env, "last_agent_rewards", None)
            if ar is not None:
                ar_np = np.asarray(ar, np.float32)
                ar_acc = ar_np if ar_acc is None else ar_acc + ar_np
            done_acc = done_acc or done
            if done: break
        # Per-agent rewards (difference rewards) take precedence when present.
        if ar_acc is not None:
            B["adv"][t] = ar_acc
        else:
            B["adv"][t] = r_acc
        t += 1
        if done_acc:
            break
    # Effective decision-level discount and GAE trace decay.
    gamma_eff = float(agent_gamma(agent)) ** int(action_repeat)
    lam_eff = float(agent_lam(agent)) ** int(action_repeat)
    # Distinguish a genuine terminal state from a truncation.
    #
    # The environment ends an episode when the mission time limit is reached.
    # That is a TRUNCATION, not a terminal state: the value of the final state
    # is still meaningful, so the GAE recursion must bootstrap from V(s_{t+1}).
    # Conflating the two biases every value target downward.  The environment
    # therefore reports `terminated` separately, and the rollout trusts it
    # rather than inferring termination from `done`.
    env_terminated = bool(getattr(env, "terminated", False))
    truncated = not env_terminated
    terminal_state = state if truncated else None

    # ---- GAE (26) with bootstrap at truncation -----------------------
    # At a natural termination, the bootstrap value is 0. At a truncation
    # (hit the horizon without done), we must bootstrap with V(s_{t+1}) so
    # the value target is not biased downward. A previous revision used 0 in
    # both cases, underestimating returns at the horizon.
    adv = np.zeros_like(B["adv"])
    last_gae = np.zeros(n, np.float32)
    # Bootstrap value for the boundary (only used when truncated).
    if truncated and agent is not None and terminal_state is not None:
        import torch
        with torch.no_grad():
            v_boot = agent.value(
                torch.as_tensor(terminal_state, dtype=torch.float32, device=agent.device)
            ).cpu().numpy()
        bootstrap_val = np.full(n, float(v_boot))
    else:
        bootstrap_val = np.zeros(n, np.float32)
    for step in reversed(range(t)):
        if step + 1 < t:
            next_val = B["values"][step + 1]
        else:
            next_val = bootstrap_val
        delta = B["adv"][step] + gamma_eff * next_val - B["values"][step]
        last_gae = delta + gamma_eff * lam_eff * last_gae
        adv[step] = last_gae
    B["adv"] = adv
    # The batch must be truncated to the number of steps actually collected
    # BEFORE the returns are formed.  The episode now ends early whenever a
    # UAV failure terminates the mission, so t is not always n_steps, and
    # forming the returns from the untruncated advantage array gave a
    # shape mismatch (and would have silently mis-aligned the targets).
    B = {k: v[:t] for k, v in B.items()}
    B["ret"] = B["adv"] + B["values"]
    return B, env.summary()


def agent_gamma(agent):
    return agent.gamma if agent else 0.99


def agent_lam(agent):
    return agent.lam if agent else 0.95


def agent_obs_dim(agent):
    """Input width of the agent's actor network.

    Replaced a stub that returned the literal 32. That hardcoded value matched
    neither the observation layout (which used to write only 24 of 32 slots and
    now writes exactly 30) nor any configuration other than the reference one,
    so a buffer sized from it silently disagreed with the environment.
    """
    first = next(agent.actor.parameters())
    return int(first.shape[-1])


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Training
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
def _domain_env(base_cfg, ep, episodes, seed):
    """Scene mixture for domain-randomised training.

    Keeps N, M and the pad count fixed (they change the critic input), and
    randomises only quantities that leave obs_dim and state_dim intact:
    uniform or corner-clustered sensors, pack size, communication radius,
    coverage radius and mid-episode failures.  The last 20% of training is
    the target nominal distribution, so the final policy is sharp on the
    configuration the report evaluates.
    """
    import copy
    c = copy.deepcopy(base_cfg)
    c.randomize_layout = True
    if ep < int(0.8 * episodes):
        mode = ep % 5
        if mode == 1:
            c.adversarial_layout = True
        elif mode == 2:
            c.B_max = 0.5 * base_cfg.B_max
            c.charge_rate = 2.0 * base_cfg.charge_rate
        elif mode == 3:
            c.R_comm = max(25.0, 0.72 * base_cfg.R_comm)
        elif mode == 4:
            c.failure_enabled = True
            c.failure_time = -1
            c.R_cov = max(28.0, 0.90 * base_cfg.R_cov)
    c.seed = int(seed) * 100003 + int(ep) * 7919 + 13
    return UAVIoTEnv(c)


def train_mappo(cfg: SimConfig, episodes=120, n_steps=400, seed=0, log_every=10,
               verbose=True, per_agent_adv_norm=False, ent_coef=0.01,
               value_norm=True, device=None, bc_path=None,
               gamma=0.99, gae_lambda=0.95, epochs=10, n_minibatch=4,
               curriculum=False, rf_at=None, lr_decay=1.0,
               attn=False, action_repeat=1,
               ckpt_dir=None, ckpt_every=25, domain_rand=False):
    """Train the proposed controller.

    THE DEVICE IS A PROTOCOL CHOICE, NOT A RESOURCE ACCIDENT.  The
    floating-point kernels differ between the CPU and the GPU, so the same
    seed trained on the two devices gives different weights (the difference
    is small, about 6e-4 after one episode, and it compounds through the
    updates).  A device chosen by "is there room on the GPU right now"
    therefore makes the result depend on the machine's momentary load, and
    two runs of the same configuration that happen to land on different
    devices are not the same experiment.  The device is consequently taken
    from the environment variable UAV_DEVICE and DEFAULTS TO THE CPU; the
    value "auto" restores the old load-dependent behaviour, and "cuda"
    asks for the GPU explicitly.

    The choice is not a matter of taste: it is measured, and the
    measurement is in analysis/device_sensitivity.json.  On this workload
    the GPU is about 1.26x SLOWER than the CPU, because the simulator is
    a sequential Python loop that is 85% of the run, while the network is
    8% of it -- and on the GPU even that 8% is nearly four times slower
    in absolute terms, since the kernels are tiny and the launch
    overhead dominates.  There is nothing for the GPU to accelerate
    here.  (The ratio is the number to quote: the absolute cost per step
    depends on what else the machine is running.)
    """
    torch.manual_seed(seed)
    # The thread count changes the order of the floating-point reductions,
    # and so changes the weights a training run produces.  The networks here
    # are small, so the arithmetic is a rounding detail, but a rounding
    # detail is exactly what a reproducibility claim cannot tolerate: the
    # thread count is pinned here, so the result does not depend on how
    # many cores the machine happened to be using.
    try:
        torch.set_num_threads(1)
    except Exception:                                       # pragma: no cover
        pass
    env = UAVIoTEnv(cfg)
    if device is None:
        device = os.environ.get("UAV_DEVICE", "cpu")
    if device == "auto":
        # The policy network and the critic are small, and the simulator is
        # a sequential Python loop, so the GPU is used for the network and
        # the PPO update while the control loop stays on the host.  The
        # device is only chosen if the GPU actually has room for it: a CUDA
        # allocation that fails because the HOST is out of memory aborts the
        # run.  This mode is NOT reproducible, because the answer depends
        # on what else the machine is doing, so it is not the default.
        device = "cpu"
        if torch.cuda.is_available():
            try:
                free, _total = torch.cuda.mem_get_info()
                if free > 1_500_000_000:      # 1.5 GB
                    device = "cuda"
            except Exception:
                device = "cpu"
    agent = MAPPO(n_agents=cfg.N, obs_dim=env.obs_dim, state_dim=env.state_dim,
                  per_agent_adv_norm=per_agent_adv_norm, ent_coef=ent_coef,
                  value_norm=value_norm, device=device, clip=0.2, lr=3e-4,
                  gamma=gamma, gae_lambda=gae_lambda, attn=attn)
    if bc_path:
        if os.path.exists(bc_path):
            try:
                sd = torch.load(bc_path, map_location=agent.device, weights_only=True)
                agent.actor.load_state_dict(sd)
                if verbose:
                    print(f"[train] loaded BC actor from {bc_path}")
            except Exception as e:
                if verbose:
                    print(f"[train] BC load failed ({e}); training from scratch")
    hist = []
    base_bmax = cfg.B_max
    for ep in range(episodes):
        if domain_rand:
            env = _domain_env(cfg, ep, episodes, seed)
        # Innovation 1: curriculum over battery stress.  B_max ramps from
        # 2x down to 1x of the base over the first half of training, so the
        # policy sees depletion gradually instead of never (the cause of the
        # small_battery failure).
        if curriculum:
            frac = min(1.0, ep / max(1, episodes // 2))
            mult = 2.0 - frac  # 2.0 -> 1.0
            env.cfg.B_max = base_bmax * mult
        # Innovation 4: Reset-and-Finetune (ORCHID): at rf_at, reset the
        # optimiser state and decay the learning rate.
        if rf_at is not None and ep == rf_at:
            for opt in (agent.actor_opt, agent.critic_opt):
                opt.param_groups[0]["lr"] *= lr_decay
                opt.state.clear()
            if verbose:
                print(f"[train] R&F at ep {ep}: lr x{lr_decay}")
        B, summ = collect_rollout(env, agent, n_steps, action_repeat=action_repeat)
        losses = agent.update(B, epochs=epochs, n_minibatch=n_minibatch)
        rec = dict(episode=ep, mean_reward=float(B["adv"][:, 0].mean()), **losses)
        rec.update({k: float(v) for k, v in summ.items()})
        # the device is recorded with the training curve, so that every
        # artefact says which device produced it
        rec["device"] = str(agent.device)
        hist.append(rec)
        # intermediate checkpoint (survives power cuts)
        if ckpt_dir and (ep % ckpt_every == 0 or ep == episodes - 1):
            os.makedirs(ckpt_dir, exist_ok=True)
            agent.save(os.path.join(ckpt_dir, "latest.pt"), cfg=cfg)
            with open(os.path.join(ckpt_dir, "partial.json"), "w") as _f:
                json.dump({"ep": ep, "episodes": episodes,
                           "train_ck": float(hist[-1].get("CK", 0.0)),
                           "hist": hist}, _f)
        if verbose and (ep % log_every == 0 or ep == episodes - 1):
            print(
                f"[MAPPO ep {ep:3d}] R={rec['mean_reward']:+.4f} "
                f"CK={rec.get('CK',0):.3f} AG={rec.get('AG',0):.3f} "
                f"Crit={rec.get('Crit',0):.3f} ent={rec['entropy']:.3f}"
            )
    return agent, hist


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Rule-based baselines (Table VIII)
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
def _seeded(cfg: SimConfig, seed: int) -> SimConfig:
    """Copy of cfg with the episode seed applied.

    The seed selects the random scene (sensor layout, pad placement, initial
    positions and the shadowing draw).  Every baseline must apply it, or a
    "five-seed" comparison degenerates into the same episode run five times
    while the learned policy is evaluated on five different scenes.
    """
    import copy
    c = copy.deepcopy(cfg)
    c.seed = int(seed)
    return c


def _move_toward(env, i, target):
    c = env.cfg
    d = target - env.p[i]
    if np.linalg.norm(d) < 1e-6:
        return A_HOVER
    step = c.v_max * c.dt
    # snap to the dominant cardinal axis (discrete action space)
    if abs(d[0]) >= abs(d[1]):
        return A_PX if d[0] > 0 else A_NX
    return A_PY if d[1] > 0 else A_NY


def _uncovered_target(env):
    ncov = env.coverage_counts()
    under = np.where(ncov < env.cfg.K)[0]
    if len(under):
        return env.sensors[under[np.argmin(np.linalg.norm(env.sensors[under] - env.p.mean(0), axis=1))]]
    return env.pads[0]


def baseline_fixed(cfg: SimConfig, steps=1000, seed=0):
    """Fixed formation with threshold recharge.

    The `seed` argument selects the episode's random scene (sensor layout,
    pad placement, initial positions).  It MUST be applied to the config:
    accepting the argument and ignoring it would make every "seed" in a
    multi-seed comparison the same episode, which silently turns a
    five-seed comparison into one repeated run.
    """
    cfg = _seeded(cfg, seed)
    env = UAVIoTEnv(cfg)
    obs, st = env.reset()
    for _ in range(steps):
        acts = np.zeros(cfg.N, dtype=np.int64)
        for i in env._active_idx():
            if env.E[i] <= env.return_threshold(i):
                # walk straight to the nearest pad, charge on arrival
                if np.min(np.linalg.norm(env.pads - env.p[i], axis=1)) <= env.cfg.pad_service_radius:
                    acts[i] = A_CHARGE
                else:
                    acts[i] = _move_toward(env, i, env.pads[int(np.argmin(np.linalg.norm(env.pads - env.p[i], axis=1)))])
            else:
                acts[i] = A_HOVER
        obs, st, r, done, _ = env.step(acts)
        if done:
            break
    return env.summary()


def baseline_greedy(cfg: SimConfig, steps=1000, seed=0):
    """Greedy lowest-battery recharge with the same motion rule."""
    cfg = _seeded(cfg, seed)
    env = UAVIoTEnv(cfg)
    obs, st = env.reset()
    for _ in range(steps):
        acts = np.zeros(cfg.N, dtype=np.int64)
        for i in env._active_idx():
            if env.E[i] <= env.return_threshold(i):
                if np.min(np.linalg.norm(env.pads - env.p[i], axis=1)) <= env.cfg.pad_service_radius:
                    acts[i] = A_CHARGE
                else:
                    acts[i] = _move_toward(env, i, env.pads[int(np.argmin(np.linalg.norm(env.pads - env.p[i], axis=1)))])
            else:
                acts[i] = _move_toward(env, i, _uncovered_target(env))
        obs, st, r, done, _ = env.step(acts)
        if done:
            break
    return env.summary()


def baseline_reactive_local(cfg: SimConfig, steps=1000, seed=0):
    """The same reactive heuristic, WITHOUT the privileged global state.

    `baseline_reactive` assigns the swarm to the under-covered sensors in a
    global round-robin: it reads the coverage counts of EVERY sensor, so
    it knows which sensors are under-covered and which UAV is assigned to
    which.  That is an unbounded broadcast of the coverage state, and the
    learned policy receives a fixed-size summary of the same information.
    Part of the gap between them is therefore a difference of information
    and not of control.

    This variant removes most of that privilege while keeping the rule
    hand-designed and the motion logic identical.  Each UAV uses ONLY:

      * its own position, battery and mode;
      * the STATIC sensor layout and the static pad layout, which are
        infrastructure rather than coverage state and which a deployed
        UAV can be told once;
      * the coverage counts of the sensors IT is close enough to judge for
        itself -- a radius of twice R_cov, which is what a UAV can
        observe locally.

    It does not use the global under-covered list, and it does not use an
    assignment: each UAV moves toward the nearest sensor that IT judges
    to be under-covered.  The resulting contention between UAVs that want
    the same sensor is exactly the difficulty the learned policy faces,
    and it is the point of the variant.
    """
    cfg = _seeded(cfg, seed)
    env = UAVIoTEnv(cfg)
    obs, st = env.reset()
    local_radius = 2.0 * cfg.R_cov
    for _ in range(steps):
        acts = np.zeros(cfg.N, dtype=np.int64)
        ncov = env.coverage_counts()
        for i in env._active_idx():
            if env.E[i] <= env.return_threshold(i):
                if np.min(np.linalg.norm(env.pads - env.p[i], axis=1)) <= cfg.pad_service_radius:
                    acts[i] = A_CHARGE
                else:
                    acts[i] = _move_toward(env, i, env.pads[int(np.argmin(np.linalg.norm(env.pads - env.p[i], axis=1)))])
                continue
            # the sensors this UAV can judge for itself
            d = np.linalg.norm(env.sensors - env.p[i], axis=1)
            local = np.nonzero(d <= local_radius)[0]
            under_local = local[ncov[local] < cfg.K]
            if len(under_local) == 0:
                acts[i] = A_HOVER
                continue
            tgt = env.sensors[under_local[int(np.argmin(d[under_local]))]]
            acts[i] = _move_toward(env, i, tgt)
        obs, st, r, done, _ = env.step(acts)
        if done:
            break
    return env.summary()


def baseline_reactive(cfg: SimConfig, steps=1000, seed=0):
    """Deterministic coverage + connectivity heuristic (no learning)."""
    cfg = _seeded(cfg, seed)
    env = UAVIoTEnv(cfg)
    obs, st = env.reset()
    for _ in range(steps):
        acts = np.zeros(cfg.N, dtype=np.int64)
        ncov = env.coverage_counts()
        under = np.where(ncov < cfg.K)[0]
        for k, i in enumerate(env._active_idx()):
            if env.E[i] <= env.return_threshold(i):
                if np.min(np.linalg.norm(env.pads - env.p[i], axis=1)) <= cfg.pad_service_radius:
                    acts[i] = A_CHARGE
                else:
                    acts[i] = _move_toward(env, i, env.pads[int(np.argmin(np.linalg.norm(env.pads - env.p[i], axis=1)))])
                continue
            if len(under) == 0:
                acts[i] = A_HOVER
                continue
            # assign the k-th uncovered sensor round-robin so the swarm splits up
            tgt = env.sensors[under[k % len(under)]]
            acts[i] = _move_toward(env, i, tgt)
        obs, st, r, done, _ = env.step(acts)
        if done:
            break
    return env.summary()


==============================================================================
=== ml_impl/uav_wireless/experiments.py  (700 lines)
==============================================================================
"""
Experiment design matrix (Table X / Section VII).

Runs the full comparison: Fixed, Greedy, Reactive, MAPPO (proposed),
Vanilla MAPPO (ablation) across 8 scenarios x 5 seeds, and prints a
metrics table. Results are saved to CSV.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import json
import os
import numpy as np

from .env import SimConfig, UAVIoTEnv
from .train import (
    train_mappo, collect_rollout,
    baseline_fixed, baseline_greedy, baseline_reactive,
    baseline_reactive_local,
)


def base_config(**overrides) -> SimConfig:
    """The one canonical experimental configuration.

    Every script must build its configuration from here, so that the
    numbers in the paper, the numbers in the CSVs and the numbers in the
    audit all describe the same experiment.

    Choices, and why:

    N = 8, K = 2, R_cov = 35 m
        The corner-packing bound requires N >= 4K, so N = 8 is the smallest
        feasible fleet for K = 2 on this field.

    start_clustered = True
        A centred ring deployment already covers 17 of 20 sensors at t = 0,
        so every controller -- including one that never moves -- scores
        CK = 0.850 and the comparison cannot separate them.  The clustered
        deployment is connected at t = 0 and covers 3 of 20, so the
        controller has real work to do.

    episode_len = 2000 steps (400 s of flight)
        At 400 steps the pack never depletes, so the return-to-charge
        transition never fires, the charging arbitration is never
        exercised and the reserve UAV has nothing to recover.  At 2000
        steps the constraint binds (283 return transitions and 0.7 stranded
        UAVs per episode under the Reactive heuristic) while the mission
        remains achievable.

    B_max = 30 000 J, charge_rate = 0.0002/step
        The pack size and charge rate of the report's parameter table; the
        episode length is what changed.

    use_agent_id, map_grid = 4, use_pad_bearing
        The observation must identify the agent for a shared policy to
        specialise, must carry a summary of where the coverage deficit is
        (the reactive heuristic is given the EXACT deficit set, so the
        comparison is only like-for-like if the learned policy has a
        broadcast summary of it too), and must give the direction of the
        charging pad.  The map summary is MEASURED to hurt the learned
        controller at this budget -- the policy follows the deficit, because
        a location without an assignment is not actionable -- and the
        measurement is reported in the results, together with the variant
        that omits it.
    """
    cfg = SimConfig(
        N=8, K=2, R_cov=35.0, M=20, episode_len=2000, seed=0,
        B_max=30_000.0, charge_rate=0.0002,
        lam_B=2.0,
        start_clustered=True,
        use_agent_id=True, map_grid=4, use_pad_bearing=True,
    )
    for k, v in overrides.items():
        setattr(cfg, k, v)
    return cfg


def scenario_cfgs(base: SimConfig):
    """S1..S8 -> list of (id, research question, [configs])."""
    import copy

    def cp(**kw):
        c = copy.deepcopy(base)
        for k, v in kw.items():
            setattr(c, k, v)
        return c

    scen = []
    scen.append(("S1", "sensor density",
                 [cp(M=10, seed=s) for s in range(2)] +
                 [cp(M=40, seed=s) for s in range(2)]))
    scen.append(("S2", "fleet size N",
                 [cp(N=3, seed=s) for s in range(2)] +
                 [cp(N=8, seed=s) for s in range(2)]))
    scen.append(("S3", "battery capacity",
                 [cp(B_max=15_000.0, charge_rate=0.0004, seed=s) for s in range(2)] +
                 [cp(B_max=60_000.0, seed=s) for s in range(2)]))
    scen.append(("S4", "charging pads S",
                 [cp(n_pads=1, seed=s) for s in range(2)] +
                 [cp(n_pads=4, seed=s) for s in range(2)]))
    scen.append(("S5", "R_comm",
                 [cp(R_comm=25.0, seed=s) for s in range(2)] +
                 [cp(R_comm=55.0, seed=s) for s in range(2)]))
    scen.append(("S6", "R_cov",
                 [cp(R_cov=28.0, seed=s) for s in range(2)] +
                 [cp(R_cov=45.0, seed=s) for s in range(2)]))
    scen.append(("S7", "failure time",
                 [cp(failure_enabled=True, failure_time=200, seed=s) for s in range(2)] +
                 [cp(failure_enabled=True, failure_time=700, seed=s) for s in range(2)]))
    scen.append(("S8", "combined stress",
                 [cp(failure_enabled=True, failure_time=500, n_pads=1,
                     B_max=15_000.0, charge_rate=0.0004, R_comm=30.0, R_cov=30.0, seed=s)
                  for s in range(2)]))
    scen.append(("S9", "initial deployment",
                 [cp(start_clustered=True, seed=s) for s in range(2)] +
                 [cp(start_clustered=False, seed=s) for s in range(2)]))
    return scen


def eval_agent(agent, cfg, steps=1000, seed=0, deterministic=False):
    """Roll out the learned policy and return the episode summary.

    The default is the SAMPLED policy, because Eq. (13) of the report
    specifies a_i(t) ~ pi_theta(a_i | o_i(t)): the deployed policy draws
    from the categorical distribution, it does not take the argmax.  The
    difference between the two is not cosmetic and is reported: with a
    shared actor the deterministic policy tends to select the SAME action
    for every UAV, so the swarm translates as a rigid body and never
    disperses, while the sampled policy breaks that tie by chance and
    deploys.  `deterministic=True` selects the argmax policy for
    comparison.
    """
    import copy
    c = copy.deepcopy(cfg)
    c.seed = seed
    # Evaluation scenes are a function of the seed ALONE.  If training used
    # randomized layouts, leaving the flag on here would make reset() draw a
    # different scene for the learner than the rule baselines see.
    c.randomize_layout = False
    # The policy samples, and the sampling draws from the global torch RNG.
    # Seed it from the evaluation seed alone, so that the evaluation is a
    # function of (policy, scene) and every controller sees the same stream
    # on the same scene.  See uav_wireless/seeding.py.
    from .seeding import seed_evaluation
    seed_evaluation(seed)
    env = UAVIoTEnv(c)
    obs, st = env.reset()
    for _ in range(steps):
        import numpy as np
        mask = np.stack(env.action_masks())
        for i in range(c.N):
            if mask[i].sum() == 0:
                mask[i, 0] = True
                mask[i] = mask[i] | (mask[i] == 0)
        acts = agent.act(obs, mask, deterministic=deterministic)
        obs, st, r, done, _ = env.step(acts)
        if done:
            break
    return env.summary()


def run_base_comparison(base: SimConfig, out_csv="results_main.csv",
                        eval_seeds=5, train=True, agent=None, learned=None):
    """Train MAPPO on the base config, compare against the rule baselines.

    The baselines are evaluated on the SAME seeds as the learned policy.
    (They previously accepted a seed and ignored it, so a "five-seed"
    comparison ran one scene five times for the baselines while the learned
    policy was evaluated on five different scenes -- which flatters the
    learned policy whenever the extra seeds happen to be easier.)

    `learned` carries the learned controller's numbers when they have
    already been measured over several TRAINING seeds, which is the
    protocol the paper describes: a controller is trained N times and
    reported as the mean over the N runs, with the seed-to-seed spread.
    Passing a single `agent` instead reports that one policy, which is
    what this table did before and is NOT the protocol the text describes.
    """
    import csv
    rows = []

    if agent is None and train:
        print("\n=== Training proposed MAPPO on base config ===")
        agent, hist = train_mappo(base, episodes=120, n_steps=400, seed=0,
                                  log_every=20)
        agent.save("mappo_base.pt")

    print(f"\n=== Rule-based baselines ({eval_seeds} seeds) ===")
    for name, fn in [("Fixed", baseline_fixed),
                     ("Greedy", baseline_greedy),
                     ("Reactive", baseline_reactive),
                     # the same rule WITHOUT the privileged global coverage
                     # state: the information-asymmetry arm
                     ("Reactive-local", baseline_reactive_local)]:
        # Held-out evaluation scenes: the rule rows must not be measured on
        # the same seed space training consumed (0..eval_seeds-1).
        sums = [fn(base, steps=base.episode_len, seed=s)
                for s in range(1000, 1000 + eval_seeds)]
        agg = {k: float(np.mean([s[k] for s in sums])) for k in sums[0]}
        std = {k: (float(np.std([s[k] for s in sums], ddof=1))
                   if len(sums) > 1 else 0.0) for k in sums[0]}
        print(f"{name:10s} CK={agg['CK']:.3f}±{std['CK']:.3f} "
              f"AG={agg['AG']:.3f} SC={agg['SC']:.3f} "
              f"CI={agg['CovIntegral']:.3f} E={agg['Energy']:.0f}")
        rows.append(dict(model=name, **agg))

    if learned:
        # the learned controller, measured over several training seeds
        for name in ("MAPPO", "MAPPO-argmax"):
            r = learned.get(name)
            if r is None:
                continue
            print(f"{name:10s} CK={r['CK']:.3f}+-{r.get('CK_std', 0.0):.3f} "
                  f"AG={r['AG']:.3f} SC={r['SC']:.3f} "
                  f"CI={r['CovIntegral']:.3f} E={r.get('Energy', 0.0):.0f} "
                  f"({r.get('train_seeds', '?')} training seeds)")
            rows.append(dict(model=name, **r))
    else:
        print(f"\n=== MAPPO (proposed) evaluation ({eval_seeds} seeds) ===")
        evs = [eval_agent(agent, base, steps=base.episode_len, seed=s)
               for s in range(eval_seeds)]
        agg = {k: float(np.mean([s[k] for s in evs])) for k in evs[0]}
        std = {k: (float(np.std([s[k] for s in evs], ddof=1))
                   if len(evs) > 1 else 0.0) for k in evs[0]}
        print(f"{'MAPPO':10s} CK={agg['CK']:.3f}+-{std['CK']:.3f} "
              f"AG={agg['AG']:.3f} SC={agg['SC']:.3f} "
              f"CI={agg['CovIntegral']:.3f} E={agg['Energy']:.0f}")
        rows.append(dict(model="MAPPO", **agg))

        # The deterministic (argmax) policy of the SAME learned actor, for
        # comparison.  This is not the policy Eq. (13) specifies, and the gap
        # between the two is a reported result, not an implementation detail.
        det = [eval_agent(agent, base, steps=base.episode_len, seed=s,
                           deterministic=True) for s in range(eval_seeds)]
        dagg = {k: float(np.mean([s[k] for s in det])) for k in det[0]}
        dstd = {k: float(np.std([s[k] for s in det])) for k in det[0]}
        print(f"{'MAPPO(argmax)':10s} CK={dagg['CK']:.3f}+-{dstd['CK']:.3f} "
              f"AG={dagg['AG']:.3f} SC={dagg['SC']:.3f} "
              f"CI={dagg['CovIntegral']:.3f}")
        rows.append(dict(model="MAPPO-argmax", **dagg))

    # ---- the achievability ceiling ---------------------------------
    # The teleporting placement oracle is the reference against which every
    # coverage number in this table is meant to be read.  It is a property
    # of the configuration, not of any controller, so it is reported once.
    try:
        import sys as _sys
        _sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(
            os.path.abspath(__file__))))
        from analysis.feasibility import analyse_scene
        ck = []
        for s in range(min(eval_seeds, 3)):
            r = analyse_scene(s, base, iters=60, restarts=8)
            ck.append(float(r["oracle_CK_connected"]))
        orc = float(np.mean(ck))
        print(f"{'Oracle':10s} CK={orc:.3f}  (teleporting placement, "
              f"ignores energy and motion)")
        rows.append(dict(model="Oracle (ceiling)", CK=orc, AG=1.0, SC=orc,
                         CovIntegral=0.0, Energy=0.0, Denied=0.0,
                         Reserve=0.0, Stranded=0.0, Returning=0.0))
    except Exception as exc:                      # pragma: no cover
        print(f"{'Oracle':10s} (not computed: {exc})")

    with open(out_csv, "w", newline="") as f:
        # the fieldnames are the UNION over the rows: the learned rows carry
        # the seed-to-seed spread and the seed count, and the rule baselines
        # do not, so taking the fields from the first row alone loses them
        fields = []
        for r in rows:
            for k in r:
                if k not in fields:
                    fields.append(k)
        w = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=fields)
        w.writeheader()
        w.writerows({k: r.get(k, "") for k in fields} for r in rows)
    print(f"\nSaved -> {out_csv}")
    return agent, rows


def run_ablation(base: SimConfig, out_csv="results_ablation.csv", stress=True,
                 episodes=12, eval_seeds=3, seeds=1, only=None, append=False):
    """Turn off one proposed component at a time (Table VIII).

    stress=True  -- ablate on the S8 combined-stress scenario.  The stress
                    config is what actually exercises the reserve and
                    arbitration mechanisms, but K=2 coverage is not
                    achievable there, so the coverage numbers are bounded by
                    the geometry rather than by the policy.
    stress=False -- ablate directly on the base (feasible) configuration, so
                    the component contributions are measured on a task that a
                    controller can actually solve.  This is the table that
                    supports the paper's claims.

    PROTOCOL.  Every variant is trained on the same configuration with the
    same budget, from the same set of training seeds, and differs from the
    full configuration in exactly one component, so a difference is
    attributable to that component.  Each variant is trained `seeds` times
    and the row reports the mean over those training seeds together with the
    seed-to-seed spread of CK.  A single-seed ablation cannot support a
    claim either way: the seed-to-seed spread of the learner is larger than
    most of the effects being measured, so a one-seed table reports noise
    with the appearance of a result.

    The budget must also match the base comparison's, or the table measures
    the budget rather than the component.  `episodes` is therefore the same
    number the base comparison uses.

    `only` restricts the run to a named subset of variants (used to add a
    single missing row under the same protocol), and `append` adds the rows
    to an existing CSV instead of replacing it.
    """
    import copy, csv, json, os
    here = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
    # the device this run trains on; it is part of the protocol, and a row
    # measured on another device is never reused
    RUN_DEVICE = os.environ.get("UAV_DEVICE", "cpu")
    analysis = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(here), "analysis")
    print(f"\n=== Ablations ({'S8 combined stress' if stress else 'feasible base config'}) ===")
    print(f" protocol: {episodes} episodes x {c_episode(base)} steps, "
          f"{seeds} training seeds, {eval_seeds} eval seeds")
    variants = {
        "Full": dict(),
        "no-criticality": dict(lam_C=0.0),
        "no-degraded": dict(lam_D=0.0),
        "no-energy-cost": dict(lam_E=0.0),
        "no-undercoverage": dict(lam_O=0.0),
        "no-reserve": dict(use_reserve=False),
        "no-arbitration": dict(use_charging_arbitration=False),
        "no-mask": dict(use_action_mask=False),
        "no-agent-id": dict(use_agent_id=False),
        "no-map": dict(map_grid=0),
        "no-pad-bearing": dict(use_pad_bearing=False),
        "VanillaMAPPO": dict(lam_C=0.0, lam_D=0.0, use_reserve=False,
                             use_charging_arbitration=False,
                             use_action_mask=False, use_agent_id=False,
                             map_grid=0, use_pad_bearing=False),
    }
    config_hash = _ablation_config_hash(
        base, stress, episodes, eval_seeds, seeds, RUN_DEVICE, variants)
    if only:
        variants = {k: v for k, v in variants.items() if k in only}
    existing = []
    if append and os.path.exists(out_csv):
        with open(out_csv, newline="", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            existing = list(csv.DictReader(f))
    # RESUME.  A row that was already measured under THIS protocol is
    # reused rather than trained again: the ablation takes hours, and an
    # interrupted run should not throw away the training it has done.  The
    # protocol is part of the row, so a row measured under a different
    # budget or a different number of seeds is never reused.
    stem = os.path.splitext(os.path.basename(out_csv))[0]
    done = {}
    jl = os.path.join(analysis, f"{stem}_rows.jsonl")
    if os.path.exists(jl):
        for line in open(jl, encoding="utf-8"):
            line = line.strip()
            if not line:
                continue
            try:
                d = json.loads(line)
            except json.JSONDecodeError:
                continue
            if (d.get("out_csv") == os.path.basename(out_csv)
                    and bool(d.get("stress")) == bool(stress)
                    and int(d.get("episodes", -1)) == int(episodes)
                    and int(d.get("eval_seeds", -1)) == int(eval_seeds)
                    and int(d.get("seeds", -1)) == int(seeds)
                    # the device is part of the protocol: a variant
                    # trained on the GPU and its partner trained on the
                    # CPU are not a paired comparison
                    and str(d.get("device", "")) == RUN_DEVICE
                    and d.get("config_hash") == config_hash
                    and d.get("model") in variants):
                done[d["model"]] = d
    rows = []
    for name, kw in variants.items():
        if name in done:
            d = done[name]
            row = {k: v for k, v in d.items()
                   if k not in ("out_csv", "stress", "eval_seeds",
                                "seeds", "model", "per_seed_ck")}
            row["model"] = name
            # the budget is part of the protocol and belongs in the row:
            # it was dropped here, so a table of arms trained at a
            # different budget from the base comparison did not say so
            row["episodes"] = int(d.get("episodes", episodes))
            # a row measured before the device was recorded still comes
            # from this run's device, which the protocol pins
            row["device"] = str(row.get("device")
                                or os.environ.get("UAV_DEVICE", "cpu"))
            per_seed_ck = d.get("per_seed_ck", [])
            if len(per_seed_ck) > 1:
                row["CK_std"] = float(np.std(per_seed_ck, ddof=1))
            row["config_hash"] = config_hash
            rows.append(row)
            print(f"  {name:18s} reused from a previous run "
                  f"(CK={row.get('CK', float('nan')):.3f})", flush=True)
            _write_ablation_csv(out_csv, existing + rows)
            continue
        per_seed = []
        for s in range(seeds):
            c = copy.deepcopy(base)
            if stress:
                # Ablate on the COMBINED-STRESS scenario (S8), not the base
                # config.  On the base config no UAV fails and nobody contends
                # for a pad, so the reserve / arbitration mechanisms never fire
                # and every ablation trivially matches.  The stress config is
                # what actually exercises them.
                c.failure_enabled = True
                c.failure_time = 500
                c.n_pads = 1
                c.B_max = 15_000.0
                c.charge_rate = 0.0004
                c.R_comm = 30.0
                c.R_cov = 30.0
            for k, v in kw.items():
                setattr(c, k, v)
            c.seed = s
            agent, _ = train_mappo(c, episodes=episodes, n_steps=c.episode_len,
                                   seed=s, verbose=False)
            # the device is recorded with the row, so that an artefact says
            # which device produced it: the floating-point kernels differ
            # between the CPU and the GPU, so two runs of the same
            # configuration on different devices are not the same
            # experiment
            device_used = str(agent.device)
            evs = [eval_agent(agent, c, steps=c.episode_len, seed=e)
                   for e in range(eval_seeds)]
            per_seed.append({k: float(np.mean([x[k] for x in evs]))
                             for k in evs[0]})
            print(f"  {name:16s} seed {s}: CK={per_seed[-1]['CK']:.3f} "
                  f"AG={per_seed[-1]['AG']:.3f} SC={per_seed[-1]['SC']:.3f} "
                  f"CI={per_seed[-1]['CovIntegral']:.3f}", flush=True)
        agg = {k: float(np.mean([p[k] for p in per_seed])) for k in per_seed[0]}
        agg["Reserve"] = float(np.mean([p["Reserve"] for p in per_seed]))
        agg["Denied"] = float(np.mean([p["Denied"] for p in per_seed]))
        agg["Stranded"] = float(np.mean([p["Stranded"] for p in per_seed]))
        agg["CK_std"] = (float(np.std([p["CK"] for p in per_seed], ddof=1))
                         if len(per_seed) > 1 else 0.0)
        agg["train_seeds"] = int(seeds)
        agg["episodes"] = int(episodes)
        # the per-seed values are kept, so that a variant can be compared
        # with the full configuration PAIRED: the variants share the
        # training seeds, so the difference is measured on the same
        # training runs and its spread is much smaller than the spread of
        # either row
        agg["per_seed_CK"] = ";".join(f"{p['CK']:.6f}" for p in per_seed)
        for metric in ("Denied", "Degraded", "Reserve"):
            agg[f"per_seed_{metric}"] = ";".join(
                f"{p[metric]:.6f}" for p in per_seed)
        agg["device"] = device_used
        agg["config_hash"] = config_hash
        print(f"{name:18s} CK={agg['CK']:.3f}+-{agg['CK_std']:.3f} "
              f"AG={agg['AG']:.3f} SC={agg['SC']:.3f} "
              f"CI={agg['CovIntegral']:.3f} Reserve={agg['Reserve']:.1f} "
              f"Denied={agg['Denied']:.0f} Stranded={agg['Stranded']:.1f}",
              flush=True)
        rows.append(dict(model=name, **agg))
        # the row is written as soon as it is finished, so that an
        # interrupted run does not throw away the training it has done
        stem = os.path.splitext(os.path.basename(out_csv))[0]
        with open(os.path.join(analysis, f"{stem}_rows.jsonl"), "a",
                  encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(json.dumps(_ablation_payload(
                rows[-1], out_csv, stress, episodes, eval_seeds, seeds,
                [p["CK"] for p in per_seed])) + "\n")
        # and the CSV is rewritten after every row, so that the artefact the
        # paper is built from is never behind the training
        _write_ablation_csv(out_csv, existing + rows)

    allrows = existing + rows
    _write_ablation_csv(out_csv, allrows)
    # the protocol is written next to the table, so that any row added later
    # (the extra "no-map" variant) is measured under the same budget
    with open(os.path.join(analysis, "ablation_protocol.json"), "w",
              encoding="utf-8") as f:
        json.dump(dict(out_csv=os.path.basename(out_csv), stress=stress,
                       episodes=int(episodes), eval_seeds=int(eval_seeds),
                       seeds=int(seeds)), f, indent=2)
    print(f"Saved -> {out_csv}")
    return rows


def _ablation_payload(row, out_csv, stress, episodes, eval_seeds, seeds,
                      per_seed_ck):
    """The incremental-log record of one finished ablation row.

    The payload is built by UPDATE and not by unpacking, because the row's
    own columns and the protocol's fields have overlapping names ("model",
    "episodes", "seeds"), and unpacking both into one dict() call raises
    "dict() got multiple values for keyword argument".  That crash killed
    the ablation stage once, so the construction is a function of its own
    and is tested.
    """
    payload = dict(row)
    payload.update(out_csv=os.path.basename(out_csv), stress=stress,
                   episodes=int(episodes), eval_seeds=int(eval_seeds),
                   seeds=int(seeds), per_seed_ck=list(per_seed_ck))
    return payload


def _ablation_config_hash(base, stress, episodes, eval_seeds, seeds, device,
                          variants):
    config = base.dict()
    config.pop("seed", None)
    if stress:
        config.update(failure_enabled=True, failure_time=500, n_pads=1,
                      B_max=15_000.0, charge_rate=0.0004,
                      R_comm=30.0, R_cov=30.0)
    protocol = dict(config=config, stress=bool(stress),
                    episodes=int(episodes), eval_seeds=int(eval_seeds),
                    seeds=int(seeds), device=str(device),
                    variants=variants, measurement_schema=2)
    encoded = json.dumps(protocol, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"))
    return hashlib.sha256(encoded.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()


def _write_ablation_csv(out_csv, rows):
    """Write the ablation CSV, preserving the column order of the rows."""
    import csv
    if not rows:
        return
    fields = []
    for r in rows:
        for k in r:
            if k not in fields:
                fields.append(k)
    with open(out_csv, "w", newline="") as f:
        w = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=fields)
        w.writeheader()
        for r in rows:
            w.writerow({k: r.get(k, "") for k in fields})


def c_episode(cfg):
    """The episode length of a configuration (a small helper for the logs)."""
    return int(getattr(cfg, "episode_len", 0))


def run_learned_baselines(base: SimConfig, out_csv="results_learned_baselines.csv",
                          episodes=12, eval_seeds=3, seeds=2):
    """The learned baselines of Table VI, evaluated on the same scenes.

    These are implementations of the METHOD FAMILIES the comparison table
    names -- a value-based multi-agent method and a PPO-family method that
    uses action masking -- not reproductions of the cited papers.  They use
    the same simulator, observation, action mask and team reward as the
    proposed method, so the only differences are the learning machinery.
    """
    import csv
    import copy
    from .baselines_rl import (train_id_dqn, train_ppo_local,
                                eval_id_dqn, eval_ppo_local)
    rows = []
    print("\n=== Learned baselines (Table VI) ===")

    for name, trainer, evaluator in [
            ("Independent DQN (value-based)", train_id_dqn, eval_id_dqn),
            ("Masked PPO, local critic", train_ppo_local, eval_ppo_local)]:
        cks, ags, scs, cis = [], [], [], []
        for s in range(seeds):
            c = copy.deepcopy(base)
            c.seed = s
            agent, _ = trainer(c, episodes=episodes, steps=c.episode_len,
                               seed=s, verbose=False)
            evs = [evaluator(agent, c, steps=c.episode_len, seed=e)
                   for e in range(eval_seeds)]
            agg = {k: float(np.mean([x[k] for x in evs])) for k in evs[0]}
            std = {k: float(np.std([x[k] for x in evs])) for k in evs[0]}
            cks.append(agg["CK"]); ags.append(agg["AG"]); scs.append(agg["SC"])
            cis.append(agg["CovIntegral"])
            print(f"  {name:32s} seed {s}: CK={agg['CK']:.3f}±{std['CK']:.3f} "
                  f"AG={agg['AG']:.3f} SC={agg['SC']:.3f} "
                  f"CI={agg['CovIntegral']:.3f}", flush=True)
        print(f"  {name:32s} MEAN     CK={np.mean(cks):.3f} "
              f"AG={np.mean(ags):.3f} SC={np.mean(scs):.3f} "
              f"CI={np.mean(cis):.3f}", flush=True)
        rows.append(dict(model=name, CK=float(np.mean(cks)),
                         CK_std=float(np.std(cks, ddof=1)) if len(cks) > 1
                         else 0.0,
                         # the protocol, so that the artefact says how it
                         # was measured: the number of training runs, the
                         # per-seed values they came from, and the device
                         train_seeds=int(len(cks)),
                         per_seed_CK=";".join(f"{v:.6f}" for v in cks),
                         device=str(getattr(agent, "device", "cpu")),
                         episodes=int(episodes),
                         eval_seeds=int(eval_seeds),
                         AG=float(np.mean(ags)),
                         SC=float(np.mean(scs)),
                         CovIntegral=float(np.mean(cis))))

    with open(out_csv, "w", newline="") as f:
        w = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=list(rows[0].keys()))
        w.writeheader()
        w.writerows(rows)
    print(f"Saved -> {out_csv}")
    return rows


def run_scenarios(base: SimConfig, out_csv="results_scenarios.csv",
                  episodes=12, seeds=1):
    """Table X: MAPPO vs the Reactive heuristic on every scenario variant.

    Each scenario defines two variants (e.g. sparse vs dense sensors). Both
    are evaluated; the agent is trained once on the base configuration and
    then transferred, which is the point of the exercise -- the policy is not
    retrained per scenario.
    """
    import csv
    import copy

    # The networks have fixed input sizes, and those sizes depend on the
    # configuration: the one-hot agent identity has length N, the global
    # state carries the sensor positions and the pad-occupancy vector, and
    # the actor's map summary is fixed.  A policy trained at one (N, M,
    # pads) combination therefore cannot be evaluated at another -- the
    # input layer does not fit.  One policy is therefore trained per
    # combination, and the scenarios that vary only the geometry of the
    # problem transfer within their combination.
    groups = {}
    for sid, question, cfgs in scenario_cfgs(base):
        for vi, c0 in enumerate(cfgs):
            key = (c0.N, c0.M, c0.n_pads)
            groups.setdefault(key, []).append((sid, vi, question, c0))
    agents = {}
    for key in sorted(groups):
        kN, kM, kp = key
        c = copy.deepcopy(base)
        c.N, c.M, c.n_pads = kN, kM, kp
        print(f"  training {seeds} policies for N={kN} M={kM} pads={kp} ...",
              flush=True)
        for s in range(seeds):
            ag, _ = train_mappo(c, episodes=episodes, n_steps=c.episode_len,
                                seed=s, verbose=False)
            agents.setdefault(key, []).append(ag)
            print(f"    seed {s} done", flush=True)

    rows = []
    print("\n=== Scenarios (MAPPO vs the rules) ===")
    for sid, question, cfgs in scenario_cfgs(base):
        for vi, c0 in enumerate(cfgs):
            c = copy.deepcopy(c0)
            # every policy of the group is evaluated on the SAME scene
            # (the evaluation is seeded from the scene's own seed), so the
            # comparison between the policies is paired
            evs = [eval_agent(ag, c, steps=c.episode_len, seed=c.seed)
                   for ag in agents[(c.N, c.M, c.n_pads)]]
            m = {k: float(np.mean([e[k] for e in evs])) for k in evs[0]}
            m["CK_std"] = (float(np.std([e["CK"] for e in evs], ddof=1))
                           if len(evs) > 1 else 0.0)
            m["train_seeds"] = len(evs)
            m["per_seed_CK"] = ";".join(f"{e['CK']:.6f}" for e in evs)
            # the device the policies were trained and evaluated on: it is
            # part of the protocol, and the row has to say which one
            m["device"] = str(getattr(
                agents[(c.N, c.M, c.n_pads)][0], "device", "cpu"))
            r = baseline_reactive(c, steps=c.episode_len, seed=c.seed)
            # the UNPRIVILEGED rule is the stronger of the two on the base
            # configuration, and the matrix has to be read against the
            # stronger reference
            rl = baseline_reactive_local(c, steps=c.episode_len, seed=c.seed)
            # a short description of the variant for the CSV
            variant = (f"N={c.N} M={c.M} Rcov={c.R_cov:.0f} "
                       f"Rcomm={c.R_comm:.0f} pads={c.n_pads} "
                       f"Bmax={c.B_max:.0f} fail={c.failure_enabled} "
                       f"clustered={c.start_clustered}")
            print(f"{sid}.{vi} ({question:16s}) {variant}  "
                  f"MAPPO CK={m['CK']:.3f} SC={m['SC']:.3f} "
                  f"CI={m['CovIntegral']:.3f} | "
                  f"Reactive CK={r['CK']:.3f} SC={r['SC']:.3f} | "
                  f"Reactive-local CK={rl['CK']:.3f}", flush=True)
            rows.append(dict(scenario=sid, variant=vi, question=question,
                             config=variant,
                             mappo_CK=m["CK"], mappo_SC=m["SC"],
                             mappo_AG=m["AG"],
                             mappo_CovIntegral=m["CovIntegral"],
                             # the protocol the row was measured under, and
                             # the per-training-seed values it came from:
                             # a row that does not say how it was measured
                             # cannot be checked
                             train_seeds=m.get("train_seeds"),
                             per_seed_CK=m.get("per_seed_CK"),
                             CK_std=m.get("CK_std"),
                             device=m.get("device"),
                             episodes=int(episodes),
                             react_CK=r["CK"], react_SC=r["SC"],
                             react_AG=r["AG"],
                             react_CovIntegral=r["CovIntegral"],
                             reactlocal_CK=rl["CK"], reactlocal_SC=rl["SC"],
                             reactlocal_AG=rl["AG"],
                             reactlocal_CovIntegral=rl["CovIntegral"]))
    with open(out_csv, "w", newline="") as f:
        w = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=list(rows[0].keys()))
        w.writeheader()
        w.writerows(rows)
    print(f"Saved -> {out_csv}")
    return rows

==============================================================================
=== ml_impl/uav_wireless/runner.py  (260 lines)
==============================================================================
"""
Training runner with checkpointing, resumption, and a results journal.

Why this exists: the first version trained inside ad-hoc `python -c` calls, so
no run was reproducible and no result could be re-verified. Every number that
goes into the report must be traceable to a saved checkpoint plus a saved
metrics file. This module is the only sanctioned way to produce those numbers.

Usage:
    python -m uav_wireless.runner train  --steps 10000 --iters 60 --tag v2
    python -m uav_wireless.runner eval   --ckpt runs/v2/best.pt --seeds 5
    python -m uav_wireless.runner baselines --tag v2
    python -m uav_wireless.runner ablate --tag v2
    python -m uav_wireless.runner matrix --tag v2
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import copy
import dataclasses
import json
import os
import time

import numpy as np
import torch

from .env import SimConfig, UAVIoTEnv
from .mappo import MAPPO
from .train import collect_rollout, baseline_fixed, baseline_greedy, baseline_reactive


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Run directory / journal
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
def run_dir(tag: str) -> str:
    d = os.path.join("runs", tag)
    os.makedirs(d, exist_ok=True)
    return d


def journal(tag: str, record: dict):
    """Append one JSON line to the run's metrics journal."""
    path = os.path.join(run_dir(tag), "journal.jsonl")
    with open(path, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        f.write(json.dumps(record) + "\n")


def read_journal(tag: str):
    path = os.path.join(run_dir(tag), "journal.jsonl")
    if not os.path.exists(path):
        return []
    with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
        return [json.loads(l) for l in f if l.strip()]


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# Train
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------
def train(
    cfg: SimConfig,
    steps: int = 10_000,
    iters: int = 60,
    tag: str = "v2",
    seed: int = 0,
    resume: bool = True,
    device: str | None = None,
    log_every: int = 5,
):
    """
    One iteration = one full episode of `steps` control steps, followed by a
    PPO update on that episode's rollout. Checkpoints are written every
    iteration so a crash costs at most one episode.
    """
    if device is None:
        device = "cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu"
    torch.manual_seed(seed)
    np.random.seed(seed)

    cfg = copy.deepcopy(cfg)
    cfg.episode_len = steps
    cfg.seed = seed

    d = run_dir(tag)
    ckpt_path = os.path.join(d, "last.pt")
    best_path = os.path.join(d, "best.pt")

    env = UAVIoTEnv(cfg)
    agent = MAPPO(n_agents=cfg.N, obs_dim=env.obs_dim,
                  state_dim=env.state_dim, device=device)

    start_it = 0
    best_ck = -1.0
    if resume and os.path.exists(ckpt_path):
        state = torch.load(ckpt_path, map_location=device)
        agent.actor.load_state_dict(state["actor"])
        agent.critic.load_state_dict(state["critic"])
        if "actor_opt" in state:
            agent.actor_opt.load_state_dict(state["actor_opt"])
            agent.critic_opt.load_state_dict(state["critic_opt"])
        elif "opt" in state:
            agent.actor_opt.load_state_dict(state["opt"])
            agent.critic_opt.load_state_dict(state["opt"])
        start_it = state["iter"] + 1
        best_ck = state.get("best_ck", -1.0)
        print(f"[resume] {ckpt_path} at iteration {start_it}")

    print(f"[train] device={device} steps/ep={steps} iters={iters} tag={tag}")
    t0 = time.time()

    for it in range(start_it, iters):
        B, summ = collect_rollout(env, agent, steps)
        losses = agent.update(B)
